# Provenance: what each part of the Hamilton rests on

The ledger of where every outline, size and place in the Hamilton model comes from, read at `eda059c8` (18 October 2026, after release 3.13.01). It refreshes `SHAPE-PASS.md`'s ledgers of 4 October 2026: two weeks of measurement on video have changed most rows since. It was made for the owner's question whether to rebuild the Hamilton with photographs and video first and the manual second: it says what is measured, through which camera or frame, how sure, and what is left.

It is a read-only triage of the code's comments, the model README ("How the layout was measured", "Estimated, not from the manual"), `References/VIDEOS.md`, `RESOLVED.md`, `Review-results.md`, `OPEN-QUESTIONS.md` and the git history; nothing was measured or changed for it. Each row's provenance is what those say. Claims checked against the code while writing it: the upper train bridge's notch centre is 1.09 mm from `L.Fu` (`PR([11.46,-17.47])` = (15.35, −14.18)), not the README's 2.3.

## Key

- **Outline / Sizes / Place**: **M** measured (cited), **F** after a manual figure, **E** estimated (by eye, for clearance), **G** a generic stock shape, **Fn** set by the escapement's or train's arithmetic.
- **Frame**: how the reading got into the model's millimetres. `V-face` a video frame face-on, through a homography, rimfit or anchor on the part's own holes or rim; `V-obl` a video frame oblique (a fitted camera, framecam, hand-held); `V-side` side-on video scaled by a known length; `P-top` the top-view photograph (through `p3map.json` / `PT()`: the photo-fit era); `P-side` the side photograph (18.93 px/mm); `P-other` another photograph; `Fig` a manual figure scaled; `Text` a number the manual states (Op., Table, parts list); `Lit` a patent, catalogue or listing; `Calc` arithmetic; `—` none.
- **Certainty**: **sure** two independent readings agree, or a source states it; **likely** one clean reading, its error bounded; **rough** 10-30 % from scale or view; **inferred** no reading.

Frames are `KLUwI2UUCMQ` (C Spinner's restoration, 4K) unless marked.

## 20 October 2026: the ledger worked through

Every remaining item of the order of work below, the figures' re-derivation candidates and the handover's list was taken up on `claude/hamilton-ledger`; each item's status is in `PLAN-hamilton-ledger.md`, the measurements in `E:\mc-video\oq\ledger\<item>\REPORT.md`. Rows changed: 108-43 (the barrel bridge's cock-side horn end, a measured circle), 108-10a and 108-8 (the hairspring stud's holes), 110-10 (the trip spring's bracket, 1.05 tall) with the lock at Op. 85's 6.0°, 109-14 (the winding ratchet, 40 teeth), the bezel's knurl (107), the case support brackets (107-4/5/6, bent straps) and every Fig. 106 row (the box and gimbals rebuilt from the Jones-Horan photographs). Held as drawn: the barrel's place, the train bridge's and pillar plate's holes, the mounting ring's flange, the dial feet's radius, the fusee's turn pitch, the fusee wheel's level. Open, with the reading that conflicts: the barrel's level against the centre wheel's, the winding ratchet's tips against the pawls' spring bed, the fusee cone (two videos disagree), the hairspring's height against the hub, the dust seal's column, the trip spring bracket's place along the detent (since taken up: the detent rebuilt to its measured lengths, rows 110-7 to 110-13), the case bracket's bushing, the escape lower endstone cap (owner's trace, task 08). The summary's counts above were recounted on 25 October 2026.

## The order of work this suggests

Not a rebuild from nothing and not a wholesale re-datum (the backbone's estimate: 6-8 sessions for one frame for everything, against 3-5 for this order, which moves only what is out):

1. *Done 18 October 2026: "Step 1: the plate against the frames", below.* **Regenerate the specs** to the current `L` and `PT` (`tools/anchors`, the rimfit specs, `video.py`'s table, `lower_bridge.py`), then tabulate every plate-face feature on one frame against the model. One session.
2. **The rulers and the photographed group.** The train bridge's rim against the plate's 87.57 (wcYqdgpyggQ 19:30-20:40, 36:15); the flange's 95.9 through the rimfit cameras on 9:03 and 40:08; the fusee and barrel fitted on the bare-plate frames (34:30, 36:15, 14:45, 40:08) instead of through `PT`; pillar 1 (170.3° against the video's 166.3°) and the third's bushing (1.2-1.3 mm off `L.T` on the bridge frames); then `PT` re-fitted on what is left. One to two sessions; every check table ripples (the backbone's list).
3. *Partly done 18 October 2026 ("Step 3", below): the escape passage and seats hold; the bridge rim's r 40.5 holds to about 1 mm (36:01) and `CK_Q` is the screw's hole; the cock's re-fit needs its holes measured on 41:58 through a camera of its own first.* **The stale and mislabelled fits.** The cock's top and the stud's holes re-fitted at `CK_Q` (41:58); `TB_KEY`'s escape passage and the seats (23:30, 13:44); the fourth's upper setting (13:49.5 through framecam).
4. **What only the top-view or side photograph gives**: the set-up cover, the click's pivot (8.95 on both photographs, 9.4 on 11:58), the barrel bridge's far horn (13:04.4), the endstone cap's direction, the fusee cone and heights side-on (R-DrJz1DYOY 1:12-2:48, SNLqsS9wrrM 4:16), the case rim and bezel (48:30, 49:00), the dial feet's radius (45.9; the ray readings imply 46.6-47.0).
5. **The box and gimbals** from the Jones-Horan photographs (lot 63662) and 0:20-1:28.
6. Then the visible small parts each section ranks: the bezel's knurl, the hands' side profile, the hairspring's height, the trip-spring bracket's height, the dust seal, the train wheels' thicknesses.

## Step 1: the plate against the frames (18 October 2026)

The specs brought to the current model, and every feature on the pillar plate's face read on each plate frame against it, the camera anchored on as few model points as fix it (`rimfit.py`'s new `check`: model places printed against the frame, not fitted).

Regenerated: `rimfit_34-30.json`, `rimfit_36-01.json`, `rimfit_36-15.json` (anchors on the current `L`; checks), `rimfit_40-08.json` (the ring's bore r 39.0, `MR_RI`, where it had 40.2), `anchors/KLUwI2UUCMQ_13-44.json` (the barrel cut r 21.0 about `TB_CUT`, the map's B, E and F, and its holes renamed h1 and h20 after `holes_trainBridge.json`'s regeneration: it had stopped running), `anchors/KLUwI2UUCMQ_6-29.json` (each point at the model's place for its feature: it fits to 13.8 px rms where it gave 38), `video.py`'s point table and `plate`'s F, `lower_bridge.py`'s pillar and far barrel-bridge screw.

**34:30** (the bare plate, train side; rim 0.14 mm rms over 38 points), anchored on the centre bushing and the escape lower setting alone, over f 5000-7000:

| F (the fourth's) | (0, 21.6) | (0.11…0.16, 21.67…21.53) | 0.13-0.18 |
| Fusee bushing | (14.31, −14.52) | (14.63, −14.41…−14.27) | 0.34-0.40 |
| Barrel bushing | (−22.14, −5.35) | (−22.61…−22.90, −5.1) | 0.51-0.80 |
| Minute wheel's stud | (11.30, 0) | (11.3, 0.24…0.31) | 0.24-0.31 |
| Indicator stud's end | (0, −22.9) | (0.68…0.74, −22.95) | 0.68-0.75 |
| Pillar 1 (`PILLARS.train[1]`), its foot | (5.82, 34.03), 170.3° | (5.6, 33.2…33.6), 170.4° | 0.47-0.83 |

The feet are picked as the middle of each foot's collar where it stands on the face (about ±15 px, ±0.7 mm). The spec's pillar tops, 16.8 mm up, land 5 mm out round the rim, in opposite directions: this oblique camera's error carried up their height, not the pillars. The fourth pillar's foot, read by eye at the frame's top edge, is 4 mm out radially and not a reading.

**The other frames**: 36:01 (the train bridge upturned, rim 0.03 mm rms) holds C, T and F to 0.1-0.3 mm; its cap, 9.3 mm off the face, 1.45 mm. 40:08 (the dial side, the ring's bore as the rim) puts the indicator stud 23.6-23.7 mm from the centre against the model's and 34:30's 22.9, and scatters 1-2 mm and ±4°: its scale or focal length is off. 36:15, tilted 45°, puts the centre 1.1 mm off its own anchor and fusee to barrel at 40.2 mm (the model 37.59, 34:30 38.5).

What it settles:

1. **The best plate frame agrees with the model to 0.2-0.8 mm everywhere on the face**, the photographed group (fusee, barrel) as well as the train. A re-datum on these frames has nothing to move by more than its own error, so step 2 narrows to the barrel bushing's 0.5-0.8 (radially out, as 34:30 and the backbone's 38.1-38.7 fusee-barrel both say) and what only the photographs give.
2. **Pillar 1 is where the model has it** (seam 1): its foot at 170.4° on 34:30 against the model's 170.3°; 40:08's 166.3° is that frame's ±4°.
3. **The "recurring 4-5° rotation" (seam 2) is the weaker frames' scatter**, not a turn of the layout: on 34:30 the features' angles about the centre scatter both ways, −1.1° to +1.9° (the larger near the centre, where 0.3 mm is 1.5°), with no common turn.
4. The 6:29 fit, with every point at its current place, leaves the setup cover's two screws (still `PB()` from the top-view photograph) 31 and 43 px out, about 1.2-1.7 mm, where the four video-placed points are 5-30 px. On that camera (±3 mm near the rim) it is a lead for step 4, not a reading.

## Step 3: the stale fits (18 October 2026)

**The train bridge's escape passage holds.** On 23:30, drawn through the anchor's homography (`anchors/KLUwI2UUCMQ_23-30.json`: rim 0.34, barrel cut 0.06 mm rms), the train bridge's own opening round the escape arbor, where its frosted face ends, lies r 6.5-7.2 about `L.E`, on the model's r 6.89. The r 4.71 lobe `train_bridge.py` traced (`KEY23['escape']`, its centre 1.33 mm from `L.E`) is the blue disc seen through the passage: an opening in the balance lower bridge's slab, 9 mm lower, distorted by the face's homography (the bridge is on the lower bridge at 23:30: its balance setting shows in the lobe above). 10:06-10:13 has the escape wheel lifted out from above with the lower bridge in place, which needs the passage wider than the wheel. The escape bar's seats match the two recessed pockets either side to about 0.5 mm by eye. Nothing moved; `train_bridge.py`'s note corrected.

**The cock's top and the stud's holes: not re-fitted; a 2.4 mm conflict found instead.** The re-fit was run as the 12 October trace was made (`E:\mc-video\oq\cockfix`, copied: `trace4158.py`, `final_tr.py`, `assemble.py`), with only the hole layout changed: the counterbore at `CK_Q` (22.01, 24.87) and the stud row from 6:29 registered again on it (each hole moves 0.56-0.63 mm, about 3° about the staff, the same over f 4000-6000; `SPHc` (5.63, 6.97), `SPHb` (9.66, 7.64), `SPHa` (13.59, 8.33)). Then:

- On 41:58 the holes alone put the cock's rim arc at **r 37.69 ± 0.12** about the movement's centre, where the cock's outer wall stands on the rim at r 40.1 (`COCK_POLY`); with the old layout (the counterbore at `CK_Q0`, the top-view photograph's) they put it at 40.02. Holding the rim to r 40.1 leaves the holes 0.17-0.59 mm off (0.08-0.28 with the old layout).
- The cock's own frames measure the screw's distance from the staff (on each frame's own scale: the cap's screws 6.66 apart, the stud's holes 4.0): **41:58** 25.2 (by the cap) and 24.3 (by the stud), **photo-cock-top.png** 26.0 and 26.1, **6:29** (oblique) 25.7-26.05. The model: `CK_Q` 25.08 from `L.B` (the tapped hole in the train bridge, 23:30, 13:49.5 and another movement, its spread 1.7 mm along z), `CK_Q0` 26.79.

Followed further the same day (step 2's ruler first, then the cock again):

- **The train bridge's rim holds at r 40.5 about the centre, to about 1 mm** (*withdrawn the same day: 13:49.5 mapped on the plate's holes puts it about r 39.5; "The mapping test", below*). On 36:01 (sharp, 508 rim points at 0.03 mm rms, rimfit's camera with the centre free) the rim's centre lands 0.8-1.2 mm from the centre arbor with C, T and F fitting 0.1-0.3 mm at r 40.5; at r 38.4 they fit worse (0.1-0.6). 23:30 through a camera fitted on five holes the plate places (the centre and third bushings, the three pillar screws' holes; the rim left out) puts the rim at r 38.3-38.5 centred 3-4 mm off the arbor, and the barrel cut concentric with the barrel (r 19.5-19.9 about (−22.4, −5.3), where the rim-held anchor has r 21.0 about `TB_CUT`, 2 mm off the arbor); 23:30 is soft and wide (f 2800-4000) and the holes are picked by eye, so 36:01 stands, and the barrel cut's centre is a lead.
- **`CK_Q` is the screw's hole.** On 23:30 the three holes under the cock land at (27.28, 14.04), (21.48, 23.60) and (12.57, 31.41) through the anchor: the middle one 1.4 mm from `CK_Q`, the outer two each about 1.5 mm from the steady pins' places (`S.ckp` from `CK_Q`). 41:58's own scale gives the counterbore 24.3-25.2 mm from the staff, with `CK_Q`'s 25.08.
- **The "2.4 mm" is an extrapolation.** Held to the rim (as the 12 October trace was), the new layout fits the holes to 0.17-0.59 mm (0.08-0.28 with the old). But the re-fitted outline keeps the straight edge (within 0.2 mm) and the nose (0.5) and swings the concave edge 1.3-4.1 mm toward the opening, its tip from 75° to 81°, against the 76° the rim's trace and `COCK_POLY` both give. The cap's direction (`EPa`, the top-view photograph's) and the stud row (6:29, oblique, registered on the counterbore) were measured round the old screw place; moving the counterbore alone shears the fit.
- 41:58 placed rigidly (scaled by the cap's screws or the stud row, the staff on `L.B`, the counterbore toward `CK_Q`) doesn't hold together: the rim scatters ±1.3-2.8 mm and the tip lands at 53-66°. The frame is not face-on enough for a similarity.

- **41:58 through a camera of its own** (`E:\mc-video\oq\cockfix`'s picks and outline; the fits kept in `E:\mc-video\oq\cockfix2`: `cam4158.py` (run with `MIR=1`), `refit.py`, the re-trace in `new\`, the 23:30 ruler `ruler2330cam.py`): pose free at fixed f, held only to the cock's own sizes (the counterbore's top edge r 3.7 and the setting's r 2.2, traced by eye on gridded crops, about ±5 px; the cap's screw holes 6.66 apart; the stud's holes 4.0 apart) and its rim arc r 40.1 about a free centre. It fits (rim 0.07-0.08 mm rms, the cap 6.67-6.79, the stud 3.98-4.14) with the top face seen unmirrored, tilted 45-47°, and, placed by the staff on `L.B` and the counterbore toward the screw: the cap's two holes centre on the staff to 0.16 mm and lie at 17.9° (`EPa` gives 17.1°: the top-view photograph's direction holds); the stud holes land at (5.6, 7.7), (9.7, 8.3), (13.6, 8.7) (f 6000), 0.4-0.8 mm from the model's; the counterbore lies **25.2-26.8 mm from the staff as f goes 4000-20000**, the fit improving all the way (26.5-26.8 at f 10000-20000, where the rim's centre lands about 1 mm from the movement's). The "25.2 by the cap" above was a flat pixel ratio on a frame tilted 47°: withdrawn.

So the cock's own frames (41:58 at the focal lengths it prefers, photo-cock-top.png 26.0-26.1, 6:29 25.7-26.05) put its screw 26-26.8 mm from the staff, the model's outline with them (`CK_Q0`, 26.79); the train bridge's frames put the tapped hole 23.7-25.1 mm from `L.B` (`CK_Q`, 25.08). Superseded by the test above: on 13:49.5 (sharp, face-on), the train bridge's pillar-screw holes and bushings read both ways (the rim-held anchor, and a camera on the holes the plate places, as on 23:30), the screw's hole with them; then whichever mapping the plate's holes confirm places the screw, and with the staff placed by the fourth (above) decides between moving `L.B` and re-fitting the cock. Also: the balance's place against the train bridge's screw holes on one frame, 13:49.5 (the bridge's underside with the lower bridge's cap, through framecam) or 12:40-12:46 (the barrel bridge on); and 41:58's focal length pinned (`fcal.py` on its round features, or a sharper frame of the cock alone: 42:03, 5:51). If the staff stands 26.5 from the screw, `L.B` (the photographed group's, which no plate frame checks) moves; if 25, the cock's outline is re-fitted as above.

The first reading of these numbers, kept for the record: the cock's outline (its holes' scale), the screw's place, the balance's place and the rim's radius can't all hold: one of them is about 2 mm out. The suspects: `L.B` (the balance, which no plate frame can check; 36:01's cap lands 1.45 mm from it), `CK_Q` (read in the train bridge's frame, whose scale is the bridge rim's r 40.5 from the top-view photograph: step 2's ruler), and whether the cock's outer wall really stands at r 40.1. Re-fitting the cock now would choose among them by guess. **What would settle it**: step 2's ruler (the train bridge's rim against the plate's 87.57), then the balance's place on a frame with the train bridge and a train arbor in it (13:49.5 through framecam, 12:40-12:46 with the barrel bridge on), then the re-fit as above, which takes a few minutes.

Not reached: the fourth's upper setting on 13:49.5 through framecam.

## On putting photographs and video first

What the ledger supports as a rule, for the owner to decide: **for shape and place, a measured photograph or frame outranks a manual figure; a number the manual states (an Op.'s figure, a table, the parts list's counts and masses) outranks a single specimen's reading unless two independent specimens agree against it.** It is what the model already does row by row (point 7). CLAUDE.md's "Source of truth" is unchanged until then.

### What goes through each transform (grep of `movement.js`, `core.js`, `app.js`)

- **`PT`** (the top-view photograph's frame):
  - `COCK_FOOT` (only a label anchor, in `app.js`)
  - `CK_Q0` (still sets the hairspring stud's frame, `SPD`, line 1096, although the cock screw itself moved 2.19 mm to `CK_Q` on 15 Oct, *computed*)
  - the train bridge's serial decal (`PT([10.53, 35.06])`) and both decals' UVs (`PTi`)
  - historically every pillar, the screws and the old cock trace
- **`PTr`**:
  - `COCK_POLY` (the cock's rim arc, from `cock_outline.json`). Its tip lands at 165.6°, exactly where the video puts pillar 1 (166.3°) *(computed)*.
  - the barrel bridge's outline `BBo`: the rim built in the movement frame, the far horn `FH` measured on 23:45 and taken round through `PTri` → `PTr`
- **`PHOTO_TURN` alone (angles off the photograph)**:
  - `STRIPE_ANGLE` (the damascening, `core.js`)
  - `EPa` (the endstone cap's direction along the cock's straight edge)
  - `PB` and `P2`: the setup cover's screws `S.cover`, the setup click `S.click`, the click's tip, the setup ratchet's arc `eA`
- **`PHOTO_K`**: the radii in `PB`/`P2` and the setup ratchet's sizes (`SR`, `SUT/PHOTO_K`). The dust seal, setup cover, click and ratchet keep the photograph's sizes ×1.039 (README "Estimated").
- **`PR`**:
  - the train bridge outline (`TB_NOTCH`, `TB_CUT`, `TB_END`, `TB_EDGE`, `TB_KEY`, `TB_SEAT`)
  - `S.tBlock` (the train-blocking screw, as an offset from `L.F`)
  - `LB_*` are in the train bridge's frame too, written already turned by `tools/lower_bridge.py`
- **`FK`** (51 uses): the fusee cone, the wheel's recess, the sustaining spring, `WRT`/`SRT`, pawls, end plate, top screws and collars. `tools/sustaining.js` and `overwind.js` also use it.
- **Not through any transform** (placed in the movement frame from the train, the dial or video frames):
  - `L.C`, `L.T`, `L.F`, `L.E`, `L.Ud`, `L.Mw`, `PILLARS`
  - `S.bb` (32.90, 6.60) from 23:30 and 13:04.4 within 0.9 mm; (−22.96, 25.82) since 19 October 2026 from 23:30 and 13:49.5 through the plate-anchored homographies ((−24.85, 26.27) until then, from the 6:49.0 camera and 23:30 rim-held)
  - `S.bbp`, `S.tbx`, `S.cock` = `CK_Q` (23:30, 13:49.5 and Jd2c3x8VKsE 26:00), `S.ckp`, `S.lb`/`S.lbp` (13:49.5)
  - `S.seal` (13:04.4), `S.navySeal`, `S.bbX`, `S.bbN` (13:04.4)
  - `S.dial` angles, `S.ring`
  - `CK_TR` (41:58 through a homography on holes at their measured places)
  - `BB_CUT` (13:04.4)
  - the escapement-derived `S.eb`, `ebp`, `blk`, `dpin`, `arm` (from `L.B`, `L.E` and Fig. 90)

### Known seams

1. **The pillar 1 conflict (4°).** *Settled 18 October 2026 for the model: its foot on 34:30 is at 170.4° ("Step 1", above).* The video's pillar 1 is at 166.3° (40:08). The model draws 170.3°, 2.41 mm away. The reason given is the cock's foot, but the cock's rim end (`COCK_POLY`, top-view photograph through `PTr`) ends at 165.6°.
   - *Inferred:* the conflict is between the top-view group's cock tip and the dial-side frame. It is not between two video readings.
   - The cock screw has since moved 2.19 mm toward 9 o'clock (15 Oct). Pillar 1's place has not been re-tested since 5 Oct.
   - The lower train bridge is held 9.2 mm past the fourth (the video's 10.3) to clear pillar 1's screw. That follows the same decision.
2. **A recurring 4–5° rotation disagreement (inferred pattern).** *18 October 2026: not on 34:30, the best plate frame, which scatters −1.1° to +1.9° with no common turn ("Step 1", above); the weaker frames' error.* The third bushing on the train bridge frames is 1.2–1.3 mm (≈4.3°) off `L.T`. The fusee's angle is 49–50° on 34:30 against 43–45° on 40:08. Pillar 1 is 4° off. The fourth is at 176° from the indicator stud on 40:08's first fit. The signs are not consistent, so this may be per-frame error rather than one wrong transform. A single re-fit of the plate frames against the current `L` would tell.
3. **The notch centre and the fusee.** The README still says 2.3 mm; it is now 1.09 mm (the notch r 20.89 at 47.3°, the fusee r 20.38 at 44.6°) *(computed)*.
   - The notch is a circle measured to about 0.12 mm rms on two frames, so the 1.1 mm is real at about the 0.5 mm level.
   - Its centre lies at the angle 34:30 gives the fusee (47–50°), not the angle 40:08 gives.
4. **tb2.** The top-view photograph's sunk screw is 5 mm inside the video's pillar 2 (SHAPE-PASS gap 2). It was read through the photograph's map, which is 10 % small at the rim, so the video stands. Still open.
5. **The top-view photograph near the rim.** The rims come out at r 35.5, about 10 % small against its own inner layout. `PTr` compensates for outlines, but not for points.
   - The barrel bridge's two screws (3–6 mm too near the centre, `ee8b601`) and the cock screw (2.2 mm off) have been replaced by video readings.
   - Still from it: `COCK_POLY`, the feet radius 45.9, `CK_Q0`'s direction (the hairspring stud frame), the endstone cap's direction, the setup cover, click and ratchet, and the balance rim's 29 mm.
6. **`p3map.json` doesn't fit its photograph in 2-D** (ASSESSMENT-2026-10-05 item 10). At most 5 of 8 screws land on heads under any similarity, because the points sit at different heights in perspective. `p3fit.py`'s 3-D camera is the stated check. Readings already taken from it can't be re-verified by a 2-D map. The 18 Oct photocompare pilot confirms 1–2 mm residuals on that photograph even with a fitted camera.
7. **The oblique camera at 6:29, 6:47 and 6:49.0.** It looked like 0.3 mm "over f" but places holes on the bridges' right side up to 3 mm off: each train-bridge hole left out lands 0.6–5.4 mm off, and the third pillar screw's counterbore 3 mm.
   - It moved the barrel bridge's near screw 2.4 mm the wrong way (12 Oct; fixed 15 Oct, `2ffb801`).
   - Still resting on it: the far barrel-bridge screw (left side, where 23:30 agrees to about 0.5), the endstone cap's 6.66 spacing, the counterbore 7.4, `CK_SP`, the stud holes `SPHa-c`, and the 9 points behind the dust cap that are no longer drawn (BB_CUT replaced them).
   - Use: relative and short-baseline readings only.
8. **Stale specs and tables, which fail by their age, not by model error** (*regenerated 18 October 2026 but `solve.py`'s docstring and constant: "Step 1", above*):
   - `tools/anchors/KLUwI2UUCMQ_6-29.json`: photo-frame coordinates from before the turn, such as (29.24, −12.28) and (32.11, −4.1)
   - `KLUwI2UUCMQ_13-44.json`: barrel cut r 19.2 at (−22.56, 0.23)
   - `rimfit_34-30.json` and `rimfit_36-01.json`: anchors on the old T (−6.18, 14.75), F (0, 23.9) and E (8.504, 17.598)
   - `rimfit_40-08.json`: bore r 40.2
   - `video.py`'s built-in `PT` table (line 189): old T, F, E and pillars, `bcut` r 19.2
   - `lower_bridge.py`'s `PIL` (5.5, 33.96) and `BB2` (−23.88, 22.98, the pre-12-Oct far screw)
   - `solve.py`: the docstring credits `T` to 13:49.5 (it is 34:30), and `FE` 10.444 is hard-coded from `L` itself, so it checks consistency, not measurement
   - Regenerated 15 Oct: `KLUwI2UUCMQ_23-30.json`, `_13-49.5.json`, `holes_trainBridge.json`, `_13-44_now.json` (4 Oct). The 12 Oct escape-arbor fits used scratch specs in `$MC_VIDEO/oq/fourth`, not the committed ones.
9. **Documents that disagree with the code:**
   - README "Estimated": the barrel bridge's near screw at (35.18, 8.06); the code has (32.90, 6.60).
   - README: the train bridge outline "through `PT`"; the code uses `PR`.
   - README: the notch "2.3 mm" from the fusee; now 1.09.
   - README step 3: `p3map` "under 0.4 mm".
   - VIDEOS.md: 40:08's focal length is "3900" in one row and "f 5100-7000" in another.
   - The `stale-anchor-specs` memory dates `PT` to 12 Oct; `PT` is 2 Oct (`6d1bd378`), and 12 Oct was the escape arbor's move (`8fcc1e55`).
10. **The plate's absolute scale.** It rests on one number, 87.57 mm (a Cas-Ker new-old-stock listing). The ring's bore and the plate disagree by 4–5 % unless the bore is 38.4–39.8 (VIDEOS "The scale"). The suggested independent check (the balance rim against the impulse roller's 0.249 in, 34:08–34:16) has not been done.

| BunnSpecial `wcYqdgpyggQ` 21:50–23:20 | Another movement, 720p | The bare plate | ±1.6 mm |

**Train bridge**

| Frame | View | Use | Accuracy |
|---|---|---|---|
| **23:30** | Face up, flat on the mat | The rim, cut, notch, keyhole and holes | Soft; f 2800–4000. Anchor 0.06–0.34 mm rms; holes about 0.5 on the left side |
| **13:49.5** | Underside, held at a slant | The lower bridge, its cap, the fourth's setting, holes | `framecam` f about 6000 |
| **13:44** | Assembled, from above | — | — |
| **36:01** | Upturned with the lower bridge | — | f about 7900 |
| `photo-lower-bridge-underside.jpg` | Phone, oblique | — | f about 970, one screw fixes it |
| BunnSpecial 19:30–20:40 | 720p | — | — |

**Barrel bridge**

| Frame | View | Use | Accuracy |
|---|---|---|---|
| **13:04.4** | Underside, face-on, 36.6 px/mm | The cut round the balance (0.11 mm rms), holes, pins, pry slot | Its scale is from the model's own Fu–Ba distance |
| 12:58–13:04, 23:30, 23:45 | Flat, oblique | The far horn's cut | — |
| 6:47, 6:49.0 | Assembled, oblique | — | See seam 7 |

**What one plate frame can and cannot fix.**
- **Can fix (points on the plate face, one plane):** C, Fu, Ba (bushings), the escape lower setting, Ud, Mw, the four pillar feet, the ring's screws and dial feet, and the plate's holes and opening.
- **Can fix only with parallax taken out:** T and F (their settings sit on the lower train bridge, 3.86 mm below the train face or 4.4 mm proud on the dial side).
- **Cannot fix:** the balance. B lives on the train bridge and lower bridge. It must be chained in through a second frame (13:49.5's `framecam`, about 0.5 mm) or solved from E (9.40) and the slab's cap-to-fourth distance (10.8–11.8, weak).

**Honest accuracy.**
- **Points on the face:** ±0.25–0.5 mm per frame, from the past spreads: the escape setting over four frames 0.25 mm; the bushings ±1 mm on 34:30 and ±0.5 on 36:15.
- **Points off the face:** ±1–3 mm, unless the focal length is pinned. The lens zooms between shots (VIDEOS "The camera's focal length"). A rim alone leaves the tilt loose (13–37° at 36:01), so each frame needs `fcal.py` with round features at two or more heights, and a clear cost minimum.
- **Scale:** everything inherits the 87.57 mm listing (a 1 % error is 0.2 mm at r 20).
- **Combining 4–5 frames** (9:03, 40:08, 13:52.0, 34:30, 14:45) would likely give 0.2–0.4 mm on face points. That is about what the train arbors already have, because they came from these same frames. The gain is a single consistent frame for Fu, Ba, the pillars and the dial-side holes. A large move is not expected (inferred).

### What a re-datum would ripple into

If `L` or `PT` moved, these would need regenerating or rechecking. Run each tool to see whether it fails, then `--write` or rerun where needed.

**Model data and the layout tools**
- `tools/solve.py`: update the hard-coded `FE`.
- `MOD` and `UD.m` follow automatically; the tooth tips and the indicator wheel move with them.
- `tools/lower_bridge.py` (its hard-coded B, F, E, T, `PIL`, `BB2`, `ARM`) and its pasted output `LB_*`.
- `tools/train_bridge.py` (`TB_EDGE`, `TB_KEY`), if `PR` changes.
- `tools/topview.py` and `p3fit.py`, and `verification/topview-comparison.png`.
- `bundle.py`, `fit.py`, `unproj.py` (they read `movement.js`).
- `video.py`'s `PT` table, `tools/anchors/*.json` (their pairs name model holes), `holes_trainBridge.json`, `rimfit_*.json` anchors.
- `photocompare/h2e12055.json` and `h2e11795_top.json` (model xyz picks).
- `app.js` camera targets follow `L` (RESOLVED `cce56d7`).

**Geometry checks and their tables**
- `fine.py` `EXPECTED` (contact sizes; also `--hold`), then `contacts.py --write` → `js/contactdata.js`.
- `audit.py --update` → `tools/audit-expected.json` (coplanar contact counts), and `audit.py box`.
- `holecheck.py` `EXPECTED`: hard-coded hole centres such as (18.34, −3.86, 12.02) and `'about': [14.306, −14.519]` for the fusee.
- `bom.py`: mesh, endshake and `in`/`round`/`on` are measured live; `dev` notes; `bom.py --md` → `BOM.md`.
- `exploded.py`, `overhaul.py` (`EXPECTED`, `HOST`/`UP`), `solids.py`, `maintaining.py`, `invariants.py` (the indicator sweep from \|Fu−Ud\|).
- `placements.py` baselines and `views.py` (expect diffs everywhere), `isolate.py` comparisons.

**Physics and build tables**
- `escgolden.json`: `--write` if \|B−E\| changes even in its last bit, since `escapement.js` takes `EX` from `hypot(L.B−L.E)`.
- `gearing.js --write` → `gearing.json`, then `thermal.js`.
- `mechanisms.py --write` → `mechanisms.json` (pawl contacts about Fu and Ba), then `ratchets.js`, `overwind.js`, `sustaining.js`, `motionwork.js`, `wear.js`, `traindyn.js`.
- `tolerances.js --write`.
- `tripping.js --write` (if the detent frame changes).
- `massprops.py --write` → `gimbals.json` (centres of gravity to 0.01 mm), then `gimbals.js`.
- `fea.py` (bearing places; its `r_fea_geom.json` cache).
- `bench.js --write` → `bench.json`, then `montecarlo.js`, `estimates.js`.
- `makers.py` → `js/makers.js`, then `book.py`.
- `physics.js` `data-phys` spans, if the modules change.

**Reports and builds**
- `verification/build-verification/` (`reportdata.js`, `mechdata.js`, `fea.py --json`, `build_report.py`).
- The real-chronometers report, only if the escapement changes.
- `site-assets/social*.png` (`social.py`).
- The build: `dist/`, the root copy, `site/`.

**Not affected:** the Wempe (its own `L`), and `escplan`'s geometry beyond the centre distance.

**Cost:** `ci.py --full` runs about 41–85 minutes, plus the `--write` chain (gearing about 2 min, tripping `--write` about 2 min, massprops 30 s, mechanisms about 40 s, bom about 2 min, fine about 2½ min).

### Recommendation

A wholesale re-datum, making one plate frame the origin of everything, is not worth doing as a single big change.
- The train (C, T, F, E, Ud, Mw) and the pillars already rest on those plate frames to about 0.3–0.5 mm.
- `PT` places B, Fu and Ba within 0.3 mm of their video targets.
- What is genuinely weak is narrower:
  - the fusee's and barrel's angles (5–9° spread between frames)
  - pillar 1's 4°
  - the third bushing's 1.2 mm on the train bridge frames
  - the constants that still come only from the top-view photograph: `COCK_POLY`, the dial feet radius, `CK_Q0`, `EPa`, the setup cover, click and ratchet, the balance rim's 29 mm

**The order of work:**
1. **A datum audit, about 1 session.** Regenerate the stale specs (seam 8) against the current `L`. Run `fcal.py` and `rimfit.py` on 9:03, 40:08, 13:52.0, 34:30 and 14:45, each with an `fcal` minimum. Tabulate every plate-face feature (C, Fu, Ba, the escape setting, Ud, Mw, the four pillar feet, the ring and dial-foot holes) in one frame, with the spread across frames. Fix the README and VIDEOS inconsistencies (seam 9).
2. **Only if Fu, Ba or a pillar disagrees by more than about 0.5 mm (or more than 2°), about 1–2 sessions.** Set those arbors directly from the plate table and re-fit `PT` (or `PR`) to them. That is one similarity and cheap. Then the regeneration chain above, and `ci.py --full`.
3. **About 1–2 sessions.** Re-measure the PT-only constants on face-on video frames: the cock's rim and tip (which also decides pillar 1), the setup cover, click and ratchet, the feet radius (the strips already suggest 46.6–47.0, inferred), and `CK_Q0`'s stud frame.

In total about 3–5 sessions. A true single-frame re-datum would be closer to 6–8 sessions, with a high risk of churn for sub-millimetre gains.

### Counts

34 rows. Mixed entries are counted by their first letter. Recounted 25 October 2026 (18 October's in brackets).

- Outline: M 7 (2), F 12 (11), E 6 (9), G 9 (12).
- Sizes: M 23 (17), Fn 1 (1), E 10 (15); F 0 (1).
- Place: M 16 (8), F 6 (9), Fn 7 (7), E 4 (9), — 1 (the box, the root).
- Frame (rows using each tag): P-other 22 (3; the Jones-Horan photographs since 20 October, with 2E12055 and Bonhams lot 1128 image 2), V-obl 10 (17), P-top 5 (6), Fig 4 (11), Calc 2, Lit 1, — 5 (10). None use V-face, V-side, P-side or Text.
- Certainty (of what is measured; inferred where nothing is): sure 2 (0), likely 19 (7), rough 6 (12), inferred 7 (15).

### Rests on the photo-fit era

Re-read 25 October 2026. Since the rebuild of 20 October most of what rested on the top-view photograph (`photo-top-view.jpg`, 2E11795, pixel scales at the ring's 15.65 px/mm, not through `p3map.json` or `PT()`) is read on the Jones-Horan photographs through their cuboid camera, scaled by the box's 197 width (itself a listing's 7¾ in: at 7½ in every absolute number is 3.3 % smaller). What still rests on that photograph alone:

- 106-11 case rear pivot screw: head at least 10.6, thread 3.7. No frame or box photograph shows it.
- 106-12 case rear and front nuts: their place inside the ring (their size, 25 across, is Jones-Horan's since 20 October). The front nut's place is inferred from the photograph, where no nut shows outside the strap.
- 106-9 case strap screws' heads (7.3-7.7 across); their spacing is Jones-Horan's 35 (36.5 ±3) since 20 October.

No longer: 106-10 the case strap's length (53 ±2 on Jones-Horan, drawn 52 since 20 October). The ring's radius (106-6) came from that photograph's ratio to the plate; 0:28.7 and the box photographs (68.1, 68.9) check it. The latch parts and strap screws stand above the ring, so a scale taken at the ring's plane reads them large.

### Disagreements

1. **The box's closed height.** *Settled 20 October 2026 (`cbfdff43`): 196 (base 105, glass lid 71, lid 20), measured on Jones-Horan 63662 and 38240 at 0.994-0.999 of the width; 176 (100 + 38 + 38) before.* Left: the absolute scale is the width's (below); a listing's and NMAH's 7½ in (190.5) closed height sits 3 % under the model's, as the 7½ in width would.
2. **The box's plan.** The model is a 197 square (one listing: 7¾ in wide and deep), the scale of every Jones-Horan reading. NMAH gives 7½ × 8 × 7¾ in and RMG (British boxes) 190 × 210 × 200. The model follows the square listing.
3. **The lock nuts (42121).** *Settled 20 October 2026: 25 across (24.9, 25.2, about 25, at least 22.0 on the box photographs; TN24H 4:21's 26 agrees), r 12.5 for all four; r 8.5 after 1:15's lower bound before.*
4. **The case strap's screws.** Drawn 35 apart since 20 October 2026 (Jones-Horan about 36.5 ±3, rough). The top-view photograph puts them 32.6 apart, the video's unit (N5892, 0:36.33, 0:47.5, rough) 11-13.
5. **The straps' form.** Fig. 106 draws flat slotted plates. The photographs show straight bars, and TN24H 4:21 shows a bar about 3 thick in plan. The model has curved band pieces 1.2 thick with no slots (their lengths measured since 20 October), so it follows none of them. Open.
6. **The latch support bracket's screws and washers.** *Settled 20 October 2026: one above the other, 34.5 from the front corner, 11.5 and 32.5 under the pivot, in cup washers 10 across (63662_17, 38240_21); side by side at the pivot's height, washers 3.2 across, before.*
7. **The support bracket's outline.** *Drawn as Fig. 106's L since 20 October 2026 (a short flat plate after Bonhams image 2 before); its plan still read off no photograph.*
8. **The latch's clamping head.** Drawn 19.5 across since 20 October 2026 (63662_06 from above, 19-20, rough; TN24H 4:21 and RMG about 20); 1:15 reads 17.
9. **The key's corner block.** *Settled 20 October 2026: the concave quarter block (face r 70.5 ±3, legs 38) the photographs show; a square block before. The socket's place (54, −57) rough (±3).*
10. **The lids' hinges.** *Settled 20 October 2026: a piano hinge 158 long on each seam and a slotted lid stay, as 0:40-0:44, 1:04 and the box photographs show; two short hinge cylinders and no stay before.* Left: the stay's sizes (estimated, the Wempe's) and the hinges' screws.
11. **The ring pivot screws' heads.** *Settled 20 October 2026: domed and slotted, 12.4 across, in cup washers 18 across; flat before.*
12. **The ring's radius.** On 0:28.7 the ring is 1.23-1.26 of the bezel's radius; the model is 1.26-1.29, about 2 % large. The box photographs read the outside 68.1 and 68.9 against the drawn 68 (at the box's width scale): the model stands between them.
13. **Records, not sources** (25 October 2026):
    - The README's Sources (line 1499 now) still says the gimbal pivots have the "washer inside, lock nut outside". The code, the README's later bullet and BOM.md follow Fig. 106: washer outside under the head, nut inside against the wall.
    - *Closed: the box card no longer places the felt.*
    - The `app.js` cards for the box, lid and glass lid say `src:'photo'` since 20 October 2026; the ring's `src:'photo'` since 25 October 2026 (its pivots' height, pivot screws, washers, nuts and straps measured), and the key's text says its shape and proportions are traced, its length estimated; the latch's and key's stay `src:'est'` (the lever's plan, the slot and the key's scale unread).

### Top re-derivation candidates

Ranked by visibility × weakness. Items 1, 2, 4 and 5 were taken up on 20 October 2026 (`cbfdff43`, the Jones-Horan photographs through a cuboid camera); what is left of each is said.

1. *Done 20 October 2026:* **the box's right side, square-on** (Jones-Horan 63662_17, 38240_21): the latch bracket's screws and cup washers, the rosettes and bail, the domed pivot heads and their cup washers, the pivots' height (`RY` −7.1, 92.9 over the bottom), the base/lid split.
2. *Done 20 October 2026:* **the box's height, lids and fittings**: 105 + 71 + 20, the piano hinges, the hasps, the nameplate, the corner caps, the stay's form. Left: the stay's sizes and pivot on an open box from the side (63662, 38240 open views); the hinges' screws and knuckle count (63662_18).
3. **The straps (42107, 42108) as Fig. 106's slotted plates.** Their lengths are measured (44.5, 53); their form and slots are not. References: the front strap face-on at 0:36.33 and 0:40, the gimbal strap's section on TN24H 4:21, `photo-top-view.jpg` at 3 and 6, Leland Little.
4. *Done 20 October 2026:* **the lock nuts (42121)**, 25 across. Left: their thickness on TN24H 4:21, and a view inside the ring at 6 for the case front nut's place.
5. *Done 20 October 2026:* **the key's corner block** (concave, from 63662_06). Left: the socket's depth and place (rough) at 1:04-1:12.
6. **The gimbal ring's band (height, thickness).** Its height `RY` is measured since 20 October; the band's height is not settled and its thickness only bounded (≤3.3). References: 0:36-0:44 against the bezel, Leland Little's ring in its box, Fontaines' case in its ring out of the box.
7. **The winding key's absolute size.** Its shape is traced (0:45), but its scale rests on an estimated pipe (±15 %). Reference: the key standing in its socket at 1:04-1:12 against the box, or a listing's photograph with a known size beside it.
8. **The latch lever's plan and the support bracket's plan.** The clamping head is 19.5 (rough) and the bracket an L since 20 October. References: RMG Nos. 333 and 5674 from above (British MoD boxes: check the latch is Hamilton's), TN24H 4:21, Bonhams lot 1128 image 2, 63662_06, Fig. 106 items 16 and 20.

### Counts

27 rows (23 parts-list lines, the case split in three and the dial in three).

Recounted 25 October 2026 (18 October's in brackets).

- Outline: M 13 (12), G 11, E 2 (3), F 1, Fn 0.
- Sizes: M 17 (15), E 8 (10), Fn 2.
- Place: M 19 (17), E 4 (5), Fn 3, F 1 (2).
- Frame (a row may carry several): V-face 12, V-obl 11 (7), P-other 10 (8), P-top 4, Calc 4, Fig 3 (4), — 3 (5), P-side 2, Text 2, V-side 0, Lit 0.
- Certainty: sure 1 (0; the case support bracket's form), likely 17, rough 3 (2), inferred 6 (8). (Sure within rows: the 120, 56, 54 and 18 counts and the indicator wheel's tips' height; no row's every size is sure.)

### Rests on the photo-fit era

- **The case's rim and the bezel's diameter** (107-1b, 107-2): `TR` = r 52.5 from the top-view photographs' 105 mm, a photograph that reads about 10 % small near its rim; the bezel's outside (108.6) follows it. *Checked 19 October 2026:* 2:42 (the movement on the bezel, the plate's 87.57 as the scale) puts its knurled end at r 54.2 against the model's 54.3; LPEC109-4 reads 108 ±3 % and LPEC117-6 110-113 (20 October, `ledger\ring_dial`). The rim itself (`TR`) is still read on no video.
- **Everything scaled on the mounting ring's flange, from the side photograph** (`MR_RO` 95.9, `MR_Y`): the dial's seat; the case's band `CR` and recess `DB`, so the case's whole profile (107-1a, LPEC109-4 scaled on the band) and the latch keeper (107-7, scaled on the band's 99 mm at 1:24.5). *Held 20 October 2026* (`ledger\ring_dial`): its ratios to the bore agree to 0.5 % (rim/bore 1.234, 1.238 against 1.2295); absolute 47.4 ±0.6 (34:42.0, 34:44.4, 34:30, 2:42; rough) against 47.95, kept.
- **The dial's radius** (107-19a): the side photograph's 95, checked only by the dial photograph's proportions (another photograph, within 2 %).
- **The dial feet's and screws' radius** (107-19c, 107-20): 45.9 from the top-view photograph, *confirmed on 9:03 by cross-ratio on 19 October 2026* (46.19, 45.74; Disagreements 3). Their angles are the video's (14 October). No longer rests on the photograph alone.
- Through `PT` but checked: the key hole's place, the shield plate's scale (its 20.38 mm is `|L.Fu|`) and the wind indicator wheel's module (its distance to `L.Fu`) rest on the fusee's place, which comes through the photographed group's similarity `PT()`, fitted since 2 October so the fusee lands where the videos measure it (IDEAS.md 1.12, 6d1bd378).

### Disagreements

1. **Wind indicator wheel's size**: 35-38 mm across on 23:30 (±10 %, propped on its hub) against 30.6 from its stud's measured place, the counted 120 and the inferred 12-leaf pinion; Fig. 107's proportions side with the model (OPEN-QUESTIONS.md). The model follows the arithmetic and the figure.
2. **The UP-DOWN sub-dial's place**: the photographed dial has it 1.107 times as far out as the seconds (23.9 mm at its scale), the restoration video's dial side 1.12; the model's arbors (both from 40:08) give 1.06 (22.9 against 21.6). The model follows the stud's measured place; the README calls the photograph another movement and leaves Review-results "Elsewhere" 19 open.
3. **The dial feet's radius**: drawn 45.9 (the top-view photograph's); the video's camera-free strips put the open holes 0.85-0.89 of the way from bore to rim, r 46.6-47.0 at the model's bore and rim. The model follows the photograph; at 46.8 a foot of r 0.75 would pass the dial's 47.5 edge. *Settled 19 October 2026: 9:03 by cross-ratio along each foot hole's ray puts them at 46.19 and 45.74 ("The dial feet's radius on 9:03"): 45.9 holds.*
4. **The two squares and one key**: Fig. 8 and Sec. III use one key for the fusee's square and the hands' square; the video reads the fusee's 2.4 across (20:00) and the cannon pinion's 2.16 (40:39.0, ±2 %). The model draws both as read.
5. **The hour wheel's tips**: r 8.53 read (40:39.0) against the module's 8.84 from the counts and the minute stud's place. *Settled 25 October 2026*: re-read r 8.60 ±0.06 through a camera on the plate's holes, the minute wheel's 9.36 and its post's place agreeing with the model: the counts and module stand, the hour wheel's teeth are short (an addendum 0.4 m, drawn so).
6. **The case support bracket**: Fig. 107 draws a bent strap with screw flanges; KLUwI2UUCMQ 47:22.5-52:27.5 show it (two slotted wings, a joggled tab holding the bushing). Drawn as the strap since 20 October 2026 (`d9915c35`); the bushing's size (the tab's hole 5.3-5.5 across, drawn r 1.4) and the pivot's height on the case (about 4 nearer the bezel on 47:22.5) left open (107-4, 107-6).
7. **The bezel and the case's cove**: the video shows a knurled ring at the bezel's widest edge and a flare (2:36-2:42, 48:30), LPEC109-4 a cove under it. *The knurl drawn since 19 October 2026 (`a0342657`), 3.4 tall since 20 October (`967034a8`, 48:27.61).* Left: the model's plain cone between the knurl and the crystal and no cove under it, an estimate in place of a reading (the profile there can't be read on 2:42).
8. **The minute hand's tip**: Fig. 21 bends it toward the dial; the only face-on reference can't show it; the model draws it flat.
9. **The UP-DOWN sweep**: the photographed dial's ticks span 315.7° in 56 h, the train's 14 × 12 gives 313.6°; the model prints the train's (13 × 13 fits the dial better but runs 60.6 h).

Records out of date (no geometry), listed 25 October 2026 and corrected the same day (`claude/hamilton-small-readings`; the `SEC_L` comment's 43.0 is the photographed dial's scale at the fourth's old place, kept as history): the model README's "centre of the dial" sub-bullets still give the square 2.4, the cannon pinion's pipe r 1.7, the hour wheel's pipe r 2.3 and the hands 1.9 and 2.45 above the seat (the code's 2.16, 0.75 neck, 2.2, and 9.5 and 10.3: 2.96 and 3.76 above `MR_Y`); its shield-plate sub-bullets the plate r 36, a stop screw fixed in the case in an arc slot, and the spring's pin in the plate; its dial-proportions bullet the indicator wheel 32.5 mm (module 0.266, 17.6 mm from the fusee: now 0.2512, 16.58, tips 30.6); VIDEOS.md's "Wind indicator wheel's size" row the model's 22.4; Review-results.md's BOM comparison row 840 a "lobed plate and coiled spring"; the `SEC_L` comment in movement.js a minute track of 43.0.

### Top re-derivation candidates

Ranked by how much each shows times how weak its reading is.

1. **Bezel (42102)**: prominent in every case view. *The knurled band done 19-20 October 2026 (2:42, 48:27.61; `a0342657`, `967034a8`).* Left: its section between the knurl and the crystal and the flare, an estimate (not settled on 20 October, `ledger\ring_dial`). Trace on 48:30 (the bezel alone, side-on) and 2:30-2:42 (all on disk); the face-on bands on Renaissance Antiques LPEC109-1; draw the case's cove with it (107-1a).
2. **The case's rim (`TR`)**: prominent, from the top-view photograph near its weak rim; the bezel's outside it sets is checked on 2:42 (r 54.2 against 54.3, 19 October 2026). Read the rim on 49:00 (inside, face-on; on disk) scaled by the ring's bore.
3. *Held 20 October 2026:* **the mounting ring's flange as the figure's scale (`MR_RO` 95.9, side photograph)**: its ratios to the bore agree to 0.5 %, the absolute 47.4 ±0.6 (rough) against 47.95 (`ledger\ring_dial`); kept. A sharper absolute reading would still need a rimfit camera on 9:03 or 40:08 against the plate's 87.57.
4. **Case support bracket (42105) with its screws and bushing**: *the strap built 20 October 2026 (`d9915c35`, 47:22.5)*; left: the bushing (much larger than drawn) with the case pivots' points, and the pivot's height against the case's thread (about 4 off).
5. **The hands' side profile, thickness and the minute hand's bevel**: prominent, drawn flat 0.35. 46:30-46:45 (only 46:44 on disk), Fig. 21's bent tip. Looked for 20 October 2026: no edge-on frame (`ledger\ring_dial`); open.
6. **The dial's thickness**: `DIAL_T` 0.6 has no source; 8:50.5 reads 0.7-0.85 (motion blur, rough; 20 October 2026), not built. 8:46 (on disk: the dial's edge against the flange), 8:43-8:52. *The feet's radius settled 19 October 2026: 45.9 holds (9:03).* The inner-parts pass puts the dial's face 0.6 under the model's (6.5 against 7.14): open.
7. **The dial's numerals and inscriptions**: prominent, a font sized to the radius, not traced. Trace them on `photo-dial-hamilton-maritime-commission.jpg` as `dialtrace.py` traces the Wempe's.
8. **Crystal (42103)**: a flat disc set for clearance. Flat or domed, and its height, on LPEC109-4 side-on and 48:30 (looked for 20 October 2026, not settled).
9. **The motion work's open readings** (20 October 2026, `ledger\inner_motion`; the posts, the indicator wheel's height and the minute pinion built, `69f7504a`): the hour wheel's tips (8.5 against the module's 8.79), the cannon pinion's leaves (read to 5.6-6.0, drawn 0.3-4.3), the wind indicator pinion's r 2.2 against 12 leaves (its count), the indicator wheel's spokes and rim.

Frames are `KLUwI2UUCMQ` (C Spinner, 4K) unless another ID is named. "6:49.0's camera" is the camera fitted on that frame (the bridges' rims, the centre bushing, the third's setting and others; f about 5,000), which 6:29 and 6:47 share; its right side reads up to about 3 mm out (Review-results.md, the hole survey). PT is the photographed group's similarity (×1.039, turned 19.42°, shifted), PTr its rim-keeping form, `P2`/`PB` the same scale and turn about the barrel arbor; nothing in this figure goes through PR or FK.

Provenance as in SHAPE-PASS.md: **M** measured (a frame, a photograph, or a size the manual states), **F** after a manual figure, **E** estimated or set for clearance, **G** a stock shape, **Fn** derived (the escapement, the timing arithmetic, another part's constants, a designed curve). The letter is the defining measurement's; lesser sizes that are estimated are named in the text. One row per BOM.md line, split where its pieces differ; the cock's, stud's and barrel bridge's pins ("complete with pins") have rows of their own. The Navy's Y-arm (the default balance stop) is not a Fig. 108 line and has no row.

### Counts

65 rows for BOM.md's 52 lines: the cock, the hairspring, the stud, the balance wheel, the staff and hub, the setup cover and the barrel bridge split; the two jewels taken with their settings, the three balance screws' sizes in one row and the two upper bushings in one. Recounted 25 October 2026 (each cell by its first letter, as before; the 18 October counts in brackets).

- Outline: M 20 (20), G 28 (28), E 6 (5), F 5 (5), Fn 4 (4), none 2 (3). Sizes: M 42 (38), E 10 (13), F 4 (4), Fn 4 (4), none 5 (6). Place: M 39 (38), Fn 20 (20), E 4 (5), F 2 (2).
- Frame (rows naming each; most rows name several): V-face 42 (34), V-obl 33 (33), V-side 17 (16), P-top 13 (15), Text 11 (11), Fig 10 (11), Calc 7 (7), P-other 5 (7), Lit 3 (2), P-side 3 (3), none 3 (3: the locking arm's screw, washer and pin).
- Certainty (the defining reading): sure 7 (6), likely 46 (45), rough 3 (4), inferred 9 (10). With every part of the row counted, 33 rows have something inferred and 13 something rough.
- Changed since 4 October: 52 of 65 (47). Next action: none needed 23 (19); none possible on any known reference 9 (the locking arm's four, the cap and the collet's inside pieces hidden on every frame, the hairspring's strip thickness, the cock's wall form).

- **108-3: the endstone cap's direction** (`EPa`, the top-view photograph's straight edge through PHOTO_TURN).
- **108-1b, 108-1c: anchored on the photograph's cock screw and the stud's old places.** The cock's top trace (`CK_TR`, since 18 October 2026 the owner's 41:58 trace) was put on through a homography with the counterbore at `CK_Q0` (the top-view photograph's place, through PT) and the stud's holes where 6:29 read them; the screw moved 2.2 mm to the frames' place on 16 October (`5a3411be`) and the stud's holes 0.45-0.5 mm toward the staff on 20 October (`775724bc`), and the trace was not re-fitted. The arm's step (`CK_SP`) was mapped on 44:02 through the stud's holes at 6:29's places. `SPD`, the stud's frame, is still computed from `CK_Q0` (a parametrisation, harmless while the holes stand where they were measured).
- **The side photograph alone (18.93 px/mm): 108-1c** the cock's 14.2 height; **108-43a** the barrel bridge's 3.4 thickness; **108-44p, 108-44b** the screw heads r 2.77 × 2.0.
- Not on the list though they started there: the cock's rim arc (108-1a, the owner's 41:58 trace since 18 October), the balance rim's 29 mm (108-14a, the video's 0.249 in roller gives the same scale to 1 %), the balance's place (108-14d, PT fitted to 13:49.5, held on 13:44 19 October), the barrel bridge's rim (108-43a, r 38.8 on 23:30 and 13:03.5 since 18-20 October), the barrel bridge's far horn (108-43d: still placed by the photograph's tip through PT, held within 0.1-0.5 mm on 13:04.0-13:04.4 on 19 October), the setup cover's outline and screws (108-35a, 108-36c: 11:52 and 13:04.4 since 20-21 October) and the setup click's pivot (108-38, 108-47: 10.12 on 13:04.4 and 12:19 since 20 October; the photographs' 8.95 was read through misidentified holes); the setup pawl spring (108-46) is laid out about the barrel arbor in the movement's frame on its measured pins since 20 October, not in `P2`'s angles.

### Disagreements

Where the manual and a photograph or video disagree for this figure (and photograph against video), and what the model follows:

1. **The locking arm (108-31):** Fig. 9 swings it out from the balance, Fig. 108 draws a short flat lever about 7-10 mm long; the model's layout leaves no room to swing out, so it turns 120° inward, 13 long (Review-results.md, BOM comparison 9). No photograph or video shows the arm fitted. The model follows Fig. 9's form and its own swing.
2. **The rim's height (108-14a):** Fig. 3 draws it taller than the video's 3.5 (the heads 0.9 of it). Video.
3. **The coil's radius (108-9a):** Fig. 2 0.32-0.34 of the rim, the video 0.355-0.371, an oblique photograph 0.40. The median, 0.363.
4. **The spring's height (108-9a):** Fig. 2 has its lower end about 2.1 under the rim's top; 6:49.5 reads the lowest wire 1.3-1.55 or more under it and the spring 7.3 ±0.4 tall (20 October 2026); the model 1.1 under and 6.4 tall, set by its estimated hub and the measured stud. Neither (open, Review-results.md 25: the hub and collet seen side-on on no frame searched).
5. **The masses (108-16, 108-18, 108-19):** the parts list's 93 mg nut and 125-255 mg screws can't be the measured parts each (a steel nut 2.1 × 2.3 weighs 53 mg; the heads would need a density of 12.6-14.3). The list is read as a matched pair's (`PM` ½): inferred, likely.
6. **The click's pivot (108-38):** the photographs 8.95 from the barrel arbor against the video's 9.4 by eye, until 20 October 2026; the frames then read 10.08 (13:04.4, from below) and 10.25 (12:19, from above), and 11:52's cover hole 10.11: the photographs' maps had taken other holes for the cover's screws. Video (10.12).
7. **The cock screw (108-2):** the top-view photograph's place against three face-on frames on two movements, 2.2 mm apart; 6:29's camera 25.9 from the cap's centre and 41:58's own camera 25.5-25.8 (rough) against the frames' 25.0. Frames.
8. **The cock's top (108-3, 108-2, 108-10a):** the top-view photograph's scale reads every size there 0.83 of the video's (cap 7.2 × 4.6 against 8.9 × 5.5, counterbore 6.1 against 7.4). Video.
9. **The barrel bridge (108-43b, 108-36s):** the photograph's cut round the balance (r 17.6) lies 1-1.7 mm outside 13:04.4's; its seal screws 62° round from the real holes. Video.
10. **The cock's form (108-1):** read once as a bridge on a foot (Fig. 2; mv's comment "massive bridge from a foot at the right-back" still says so); the video shows one solid block (41:58, 23:45, 2:36). Video.
11. **The setup cover's screw holes (108-35b):** counterbored on 11:46 by eye, drawn on feet after Fig. 108 until 21 October 2026; 11:52 (the cover face down) reads every hole plain and the ends on pads milled from the plate. Video (plain).
12. **The collet (108-13):** Fig. 6 against 7:22.97 (the plate's hole 2.85 from the staff against 2.58; the inner curve dipping to r 3.07). Fig. 6 (rough).
13. **The discharge jewel (108-24):** Glasgow (1885) rounds both sides; the British Museum's cat. 117A a radius on the back; 6:58.17 a sharp acting corner. The museum and the video.
14. **The packing rings' colour (108-40):** amber-brown on the video, black on 2E11795's photograph (two movements). Video.
15. **The stud's steady pins (108-10c):** the frames show their holes empty or flush on the bar's top; the model has them standing 0.65 into the cock. The model's reading of 6:50.0 (open, README).
16. **The barrel bridge's cock-side horn against the cock (108-43c, 108-1b):** the bridge's measured circle (13:04.2/.4) runs up to 0.52 mm past the cock's traced straight edge (41:58, the owner's trace) over the last 2.5 mm before the rim, within the two readings' error; the bridge eases off to 0.05 clear (the owner's choice, 20 October 2026: estimated) until the cock's trace is re-fitted.
17. **The stud screw's counterbore (108-8):** 1.25 across on 41:58 (the stud off), 1.7 ±0.15 on 42:08 (rough). 41:58, by default (not settled).
18. **The setup pawl pivot screw's head (108-47):** a slotted head r 1.61 under the bridge on 13:04.4 (3.2-3.5 across on 13:04.4 and 13:03.5); drawn r 0.6. Not yet drawn as read.

## Fig. 109: barrel and fusee

Scope: every line of `BOM.md` under "## Fig. 109" (22 lines; split into 48 rows where a line's pieces rest on different sources). Geometry: `marine-chronometer-source/chronometer-working-model/js/movement.js` (`makeFusee`: the fusee, its top and stop-bar, the winding and end-plate stack, the barrel, brace and chain; in `buildMovement`: the fusee wheel and its recess, the sustaining spring, the sustaining ratchet with the winding pawls and their springs, the barrel arbor, the fusee arbor's square, `RB_O`, `BB_LO` and the `makeFusee` call), `js/core.js` (`MSPRING`) and `js/mainspring.js` (`MSHAPE`, from `tools/mainspring.py`). Read 18 October 2026 at `eda059c8` (worktree `hamilton-provenance`); its rows brought up to date on 25 October 2026 at `589c4e80` from the commits since (the fusee stack restacked and its lower end, 18 October; the sustaining spring's hand trace, 18 October; the inner parts measured with the measure-trace skill, 20 October: PLAN-hamilton-ledger.md's "The inner parts"). Frames are `KLUwI2UUCMQ` (C Spinner, 4K) unless named.

What the whole figure rests on (each row says which it uses):

- **Places, through `PT()`.** `L.Fu` (14.306, −14.519) and `L.Ba` (−22.144, −5.347) are the two photographs' triangulated axes (`bundle.py`: (11.59, −19.8) and (−18.56, 0.19)) taken through `PT()` (×1.039, 19.42°, shifted), the similarity fitted so the fusee lands 20.4 mm out (bare plate, `rimfit.py` on 34:30 and 36:15: 20.2-20.8; the side photograph's wheel at the plate's scale: 20.3-20.9; the train bridge's notch: 20.9) and the barrel at the train bridge's cut (22.56; bare plate 21.4-23.5). Angles checked on 40:08 (the fusee's bushing 43-45° from the indicator's stud, model 44.3) and 34:30 (barrel 281-282°, model 283°), but 34:30 puts the fusee at 49-50°, 6:29 has the fusee's post 2.6 mm off (VIDEOS.md). The 23:30 notch, read 2.3 mm from the arbor through the rim-held anchors before the photographed group was refitted (1.09 after), is a free circle centred 0.09 mm from `L.Fu` through the plate-anchored homography since 19 October (`281ec323`; README, the notch); the barrel's place holds on 13:04.4 (PLAN-hamilton-ledger.md, 20 October). Every part of this figure is placed on one of these two arbors, so it inherits that place. Nothing in Fig. 109 goes through `PTr()`, `PR()`, `P2`/`PB` (those are the barrel bridge's, setup work's and train bridge's, Figs. 108, 110).
- **`FK` (0.882).** The fusee's sizes read against the fusee wheel's tips taken as 40.87 mm (the side photograph and 27:26-29:05) are scaled by `FK` = the model's wheel (36.1, from the measured centre distance and 90/14) over 40.87. Through `FK` (`*FK` or `*k`): the cone (`rmin`, `rmax`), the wheel's recess, disc, hub and bore, the sustaining spring (`SSI`, `SFT`, `SWT`, `SRO`, `SSH`, `SSR`), the top plate's hole, screws and collar, the lower collar and its bores, the end plate's fallback radius, boss and the taper pin's length. Not through `FK` (measured in mm on their own scale): the sustaining ratchet (`SRT.m` 16.55/61: its tips 0.918 of the fusee wheel's side-on, `SNLqsS9wrrM` 4:20-4:28, since 18 October; 0.27×FK, from no source, before), the winding ratchet (`WRT.m/FK*k` cancels: tips 7.4 from 26:40 since 20 October), its screws and the tapped disc, the winding pawls, their springs and the ratchet's face (27:00 through its own camera, and 19:12/28:35 rescaled by `WFS` 16.55/14.53 onto the sustaining ratchet's measured tips), the stop-bar and recess (scaled on the top plate's screw holes, `FK` at one remove), the barrel, cap, mainspring, arbor, square and chain.
- **Heights.** The fusee's cone (6.7-15.8 over the plate), its wheel (y −6.5) and the top's radius come from the side photograph (`photo-side-view.jpg`, 18.93 px/mm at the plate's edge; the profile at 16.98 px/mm against the wheel's tips); the top turn was moved 0.34 lower on 37:22-37:26 (12 October 2026); the flanges' depth (1.13) and the turns' pitch (0.999) re-read on 20 October. The fusee wheel, the sustaining ratchet and the fusee's large end are stacked as `SNLqsS9wrrM` 4:20-4:28 shows them side-on (18 October). The barrel's height is video (33:09.5-33:35), its level measured since 20 October (h 2.0 on `R-DrJz1DYOY` 2:22 and 2:05.5, two movements).

### Counts

48 rows. Counted by each cell's first letter (25 October 2026; 18 October's in brackets).

- **Outline:** G 28, M 15, F 1, Fn 3, — 1 (G 27, M 13, F 3, Fn 3, E 1, — 1).
- **Sizes:** M 36, E 6, G 2, Fn 3, F 1 (M 30, E 11, G 3, Fn 3, F 1).
- **Place:** M 36, Fn 8, E 2, F 2 (M 35, Fn 8, E 3, F 2).
- **Frame** (rows carrying the tag, as the source of the reading or a check named in the cell): V-face 27, V-obl 22, V-side 15, P-side 5, P-top 2, P-other 0, Fig 13, Calc 8, Text 6, Lit 6, — 0 (the same rule on 18 October's rows: V-face 26, V-obl 20, V-side 6, P-side 6, P-top 2, P-other 0, Fig 15, Calc 7, Text 6, Lit 6, — 1; the ledger then gave Fig 10, counting a figure only where it was the source).
- **Certainty** (the cell's first word): sure 2, likely 34, rough 6, inferred 6 (sure 2, likely 26, rough 10, inferred 10).

### Rests on the photo-fit era

- **The fusee's and barrel's places** (`L.Fu`, `L.Ba`; every row's place), through `PT()` from the two photographs' triangulation. Their distances are video-checked (34:30, 36:15: within 0.5 mm), the barrel's place held on 13:04.4 (20 October: the fusee-barrel distance 36.5-38.2 by map against the model's 37.56) and the 23:30 notch's free circle centred 0.09 mm from `L.Fu` (19 October), but not measured directly: 34:30 puts the fusee 5° round from the model, and 6:29 puts its post 2.6 mm off.
- **The fusee's cone** (109-20 cone and heights): the side photograph, read against the wheel taken as 40.87 mm, then ×FK. Since 18 October checked on both edges of KLUwI2UUCMQ 17:49 and `R-DrJz1DYOY` 2:22 (0.1-0.25 mm in the upper turns), and the flanges' depth and the turns' pitch re-read on 20 October; the lower turns' heights still rest on the side photograph alone, against `R-DrJz1DYOY` 2:22's flange tips 0.4-0.8 lower (rough) and the grooves 0.7-0.8 lower on one photograph (open).
- **`FK` itself**: the side photograph's wheel at the plate's own scale (36.0-36.9) against the model's 36.1. Every 27:26-29:05 reading scaled on the wheel's tips is a ratio and stands without it, but the cone and the top plate's radius are absolute side-photograph readings through it. The sustaining ratchet no longer goes through it (18 October).
- **The fusee top plate's radius** (109-16, `capR` from the cone's `rmin`): the side photograph. Its hole and screws are video.
- Taken off the photographs since 18 October: the fusee wheel's level and thickness (109-9: `SNLqsS9wrrM` 4:20-4:28 side-on, 2.22 toothed); the chain's pitch and plates (the side photograph's 1.55-1.76 read about 20 % short: 2.19 on three frames, 20 October). Checked by video before: the barrel arbor square's width (the top-view photograph's 2.2-2.5; 2.265 on 12:19 since 20 October).

### Disagreements

1. **The mainspring's thickness.** The parts list gives 0.0165 in (0.419) and the British Museum's late example 0.44. The videos' coil pitch gives 0.375-0.394 in the measured barrel, and the strip re-measured on 20 October 0.388 × 1,060. The model follows the videos (0.394; the owner's decision, 12 Oct). The same conflict can be read as the barrel's size: the parts list's strip would want the barrel at 1.05 of the wheel's tips, where TN24H and Fig. 109 give 1.00-1.01 (OPEN-QUESTIONS.md 1).
2. **The barrel arbor's core.** Fig. 109 gives 0.31 of the barrel (about r 5.6), and KLUwI2UUCMQ 15:36.0 measures r 5.65 (20 October; the arbor's bearings and hook are read on it). The model is drawn at the videos' low end (4.84), the largest core on which the 0.394 strip still winds the fusee's turns in the solver: on r 5.65 the solver can't set the measured strip up (0.3-0.5 turn short), though the frames show the real spring fits. Kept at r 4.9 (the owner's choice, 20 October); the solver's slack is the open question (PLAN-hamilton-ledger.md).
3. *Settled 18 October 2026:* **the sustaining ratchet's size.** Fig. 109 gives 0.936 of the wheel's tips; the model had 0.805 (r 14.53, a module from no source). Side-on on `SNLqsS9wrrM` 4:20-4:28 its tips are 0.918 of the wheel's (r 16.55, `65e2ece0`), and the face's readings are rescaled onto it (27:00 agrees, 1.12-1.145 times the old readings).
4. **The fusee end plate.** The video gives about r 7 (18:22, by eye), Fig. 109 0.346 of the wheel (r 6.25) and Figs. 28 and 69 r 6.7. The model draws r 5.6, cut to clear the centre wheel's teeth: it follows none of them. The centre wheel's level is now measured (h 0.24-1.18, 20 October); the clearance isn't re-checked.
5. **The sustaining spring.** Fig. 69 draws a 33° gap, and Fig. 70 a narrower band with a stepped lobe. The video shows 17.4° where the tips come nearest (the owner's hand trace on 27:26, 18 October; 17.8° by the automated trace, 12.5° the first reading, which took the fixed tip's shadow and the working tip's end face as steel; 16-18° loose out of the wheel at 18:58.0), a tapered band and round noses. The model follows the video. Its banking (a split ring banked in running, after Gould and Glasgow) is a design: no frame, figure or operation shows one.
6. **The stop-bar.** Fig. 28 draws a plain bar. The video shows a step underneath (the stop end 0.8 of the chain end, the step 0.475 of the length from the chain end), and the model follows the video. Sec. IV has the chain bear on the bar's chain end, but read as measured (1.1 thick, level with the band) no link touches it. The model thickens it to 1.5 (estimated), following the manual's function over the reading. 37:22.323 (the chain end about 1.5 proud, released) conflicts with 20:01.23 and 26:23. The hard stop (26:15.57-26:20.25) is the step meeting the slot floor's rise on the stop side (25:01, 25:04), which isn't drawn.
7. **The chain's fusee end.** The manual says "pinned to the fusee" (Sec. II, Figs. 26, 28). The video shows a crook plate into a hole at the groove's end (17:43-17:49; the plate about 2.7 × 2.2, rough), in a slot in the large end's band (`SNLqsS9wrrM` 4:24, 17:49). Since 18 October the model draws the slot and the hook's nose in it as a pin; the crook plate and its hole aren't drawn. The chain's length is against a sale listing's 724 mm (the model's not re-read at the 2.19 pitch).
8. **The fusee's place.** The model follows `PT`'s fit, which agrees with 34:30, 36:15 and 40:08 to about 0.5 mm, and with the 23:30 notch's circle (0.09 mm; the 2.3 mm before was the rim-held anchors'). Two readings disagree: 34:30's 49-50° and 6:29's post (2.6 mm). Which is off is open.
9. Video against video: the winding ratchet's count, settled at 40 (18:23.5 and three more readings, 20 October; 36 on 18:20's spectral count); its bore (23:30 r 2.23-2.31 on the old 6.9 tips, 2.48 on the 7.4, against the 2.43 drawn from 28:08-28:35). The fusee cone: `R-DrJz1DYOY` 2:22's flange tips 0.4-0.8 lower against 17:49's 0.1-0.25 (open).
10. Records found stale, which agree neither with the code nor with each other:
    - The model README's "Estimated" gives the barrel cap as r 18.0 (the code 18.3), the top plate as r 9.15 (the code about 8.2) and `SMAX` as 9.3° (the code 16.9°).
    - OPEN-QUESTIONS.md's summary row "The sustaining spring's section and gap" gives the gap as 20° and the band as 1.45 high, pushing 0.82 (the code 17.4°, 0.96 and 0.28).
    - *25 October 2026:* the comment over `WRT` in movement.js says the winding ratchet's tips stay at 6.9 (the code 7.4); PLAN-hamilton-ledger.md's "Open" table still lists the 7.3 against the spring bed's 7.04, which its "The inner parts" table built as r 7.4. The model README's "Its pull" gives the set-up as 0.89 turn on 4.51 barrel turns, 0.60 turn short of coil-bound (PLAN-hamilton-ledger.md: 1.25 turns, 0.24 short, since the chain's figures were re-read). The README's and the stale-record lists above were not re-read against the code for this update.

### Top re-derivation candidates

Ranked by visibility × weakness.

1. **The fusee's cone and heights** (prominent in every side view). *Partly done 18-20 October 2026:* the cone checked on both edges of 17:49 and `R-DrJz1DYOY` 2:22, the fusee wheel's level and thickness on `SNLqsS9wrrM` 4:20-4:28, the flanges' depth and the pitch re-read. Left: the lower turns' heights (the grooves 0.7-0.8 lower and the top layer about 1.3 lower on one photograph; 2:22's flange tips 0.4-0.8 lower) on a second side-on frame: `bEWF2ivi1Rg` run down, `umslgYaJYUY` 0:09-0:21.
2. **The fusee's and barrel's places** (`L.Fu`, `L.Ba` through `PT`; all of Fig. 109 and the train modules hang on them). *Partly done:* the barrel holds on 13:04.4 and the 23:30 notch agrees (19-20 October). Left: fit the fusee bushing on the bare-plate frames (34:30, 36:15, 14:45; 40:08 from the dial side) and resolve 34:30's 5° and 6:29's 2.6 mm.
3. **The chain's ends** (prominent). *Partly done 18-20 October 2026:* the pitch and plates measured (2.19, 1.95, concave sides), the fusee end's slot drawn and the groove ended at it. Left: the crook plate and its hole r 0.28 at the groove's end (17:43-17:48), their angle against the stop-bar (74 or 286°); the barrel's hook hole (15:16-16:24, never extracted); the plates' thickness; the lowest turn's floor against the chain's outer edge (PLAN).
4. **The barrel arbor's square above the setup ratchet** (prominent in the Movement view). *Width done 20 October 2026* (2.265, 12:19). Left: its height (`dO58jf0tRUA` 0:00-0:18, `KLUwI2UUCMQ` 38:25-38:38, the side photograph at 18.93 px/mm).
5. **The barrel's core and the mainspring on it.** The measured core r 5.65 against the drawn 4.84, the barrel's other measured parts built on it (20 October); left with the solver's slack (Disagreement 2).
6. **The sustaining ratchet's tooth depth** (*its size done 18 October 2026*). Trace the teeth on 28:35 with `ratchetface.py`.
7. **The barrel's end details** (*its level, end holes, boss and the cap's ring done 20 October 2026*). Left: the cap's thickness and the ring's height, the boss's height and sink (`R-DrJz1DYOY` 1:12-2:48, TN24H 0:13.4, 16:16.5).
8. **The fusee end plate's radius** (a clearance cut against three readings). Rectify 18:22 and 18:25 on its rim, then check its clearance against the centre wheel's measured level.
9. **The fusee's large end** (*the band, rim and the winding ratchet's count done 18-20 October 2026*). Left: the winding ratchet's bore (23:30's 0.335 of the tips).
10. **The small parts the inner parts' pass left open** (PLAN-hamilton-ledger.md): the winding pawls' springs' thickness and metal, the pawls' end shape and thickness, the stop slot's floor step, the hub pipe's height, the wind indicator pinion's count and its r 2.2 silhouette.

## Fig. 110: escapement, train, bridges and pillar plate

Read 18 October 2026 on `claude/hamilton-provenance` at `eda059c8` (read only: nothing run in the repository, nothing edited); brought up to date with the code on 25 October 2026 (`589c4e80`, after release 3.28.07): every row whose part changed since, from the commits, `PLAN-hamilton-ledger.md`, RESOLVED.md and the code's comments. One row per `BOM.md` line of "## Fig. 110" (lines split where their pieces rest on different sources); the jewel stones are in the second table, "The fourteen jewels". Places are only tagged here (the layout backbone, `L`, `PT`, `PILLARS`, is another ledger's); outlines and sizes are what this one checks.

Abbreviations: `mv` = `marine-chronometer-source/chronometer-working-model/js/movement.js`; `core` = `.../js/core.js`; `esc` = `marine-chronometer-source/shared/escapement.js` (`makeEsc`: `pieces`, `fixed`, `DEF`); README = the model's README ("Est." its "Estimated, not from the manual"); VID = `References/VIDEOS.md` (Measurements); RR = `Review-results.md`; OQ = `OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`; KL = `KLUwI2UUCMQ` (C Spinner, 4K) unless another ID is given; BM 117A = Randall and Good, *Catalogue of Watches in the British Museum VI* (1990), no. 117A (OQ 2). Provenance as before: **M** measured, **F** after a manual figure, **E** estimated (by eye or for clearance), **G** generic stock shape, **Fn** set by the escapement or train arithmetic; the cell's first triple is Outline / Sizes / Place, its first letter in each the main one. Frame tags as asked (`V-face`, `V-obl`, `V-side`, `P-top`, `P-side`, `P-other`, `Fig`, `Text`, `Lit`, `Calc`, `—`). Certainty is the row's outline and sizes taken together, the weaker of the two where they differ.

Three things run through the whole figure:

- **The train bridge's rim is measured now, r 38.8 ± 0.6, and no longer the ruler** (18 October 2026, `742ddfc5`: 23:30's rim against the bridge's five holes the bare plate places). Since 18-19 October the train bridge's frames are read through homographies on those five holes (the centre and third bushings, the three train pillars' screw holes; `anchors/KLUwI2UUCMQ_23-30_plate.json`, `13-49.5_plate.json`, 13:44's picked on gridded 4K crops): the notch, the cut round the barrel, the end, every hole and the lower bridge's screws were re-read that way ("Step 3, continued") and the owner's 23:30 points fit them to 0.27 mm rms (20 October). Still on the rim-held anchors (r 40.5): the middle opening's lobes (`TB_KEY`), the horn's end, and on 13:49.5 the balance lower cap, the fourth's upper setting and the train-blocking screw's sizes. The escape upper bridge's direction (10:00, 6:49.0) is from cameras calibrated on the train bridge's holes by their circles (24 October). The bare plate's readings (34:30, 40:08, 14:45, 13:52) are scaled by the plate's 87.57.
- **PR, not PT, carries what was measured in the train bridge's frame**: `TB_NOTCH`, `TB_CUT`, `TB_END`, `TB_EDGE`, `TB_KEY`, `TB_SEAT` (`.map(PR)` or built in it), the third pillar's screw, the sustaining pawl's angle (`SPa`); the lower bridge's outlines are the owner's traces placed by its cap on L.B and its fourth setting on L.F, its screws `S.lb` and the train-blocking screw `S.tBlock` read in the movement's frame through the plate-placed homographies (19 October). PT carries only L.B, L.Fu, L.Ba (the photographed group, which holds on the plate to the frames' error: "Step 3, continued"). The train bridge's serial decal went through PT and is left out (110-19d). FK enters once: the sustaining pawl's tip rests on the ratchet's pitch circle 16.35 FK.
- **The rim-held anchors are superseded** for the train bridge (above); `tools/anchors/*`'s older specs and `holes_trainBridge.json` (regenerated 18 October with pillar 1) are what the memory note "Stale anchor specs" warns of: regenerate before re-reading anything still on them.
