Of the 38 generic outlines in the ledger, about 20 are screws and a washer (fillister heads, the countersunk ones drawn as counterbores); the rest are settings, bushings, pins and posts (plain turned rings and studs). The teeth of every wheel and pinion are BS 978 (generic by design, OQ "Estimated by design"), counted inside their wheels' rows, not as rows. All 90 rows (M / F / E / G / Fn / — for outlines, M / F / E / G / Fn for sizes, M / F / E / Fn for places, sure / likely / rough / inferred): read on 18 October, outline 23 / 11 / 1 / 50 / 0 / 5, sizes 45 / 2 / 40 / 0 / 3, place 46 / 10 / 5 / 29, certainty 2 / 35 / 15 / 38; with the rows changed on 20 and 24 October and those brought up to date on 25 October, outline 29 / 7 / 0 / 49 / 0 / 5, sizes 50 / 2 / 35 / 0 / 3, place 51 / 8 / 4 / 27, certainty 2 / 37 / 14 / 37. Certainty moved less than the letters: a part measured since often keeps an estimated depth or a hidden face (the escape lower cap's depth, the lower bridge's posts), and its weakest reading is what counts.

### Rests on the photo-fit era

Rows whose place or size still comes through the top-view photograph (P-top, PT) or from the side photograph alone, with no video reading of that quantity:

- **The train bridge's rim no longer** (110-19a): r 38.8 is measured on 23:30 against the plate-placed holes (18 October 2026). What was read through the rim-held anchors and not re-read: the middle opening's lobes (110-19b), the horn's end (110-19a), the balance lower cap and its screws (110-4 42159.bl, 110-1 20762.blc), the balance lower setting (110-3 42162.bl, 13:44), the fourth's upper setting (110-28 42161.fu) and the train-blocking screw's sizes (110-29a) on 13:49.5. The escape upper bridge and cap (110-1, 110-2a, 110-4) are now on cameras calibrated on the bridge's holes; the outline, holes, lower bridge and train-blocking screw's place on the plate-placed homographies.
- **The serial on the train bridge** (110-19d): traced on the top-view photograph, through PT; left out since 20 October 2026.
- **The mounting ring's dial feet radius, r 45.9** (110-40d): the top-view photograph's radius, confirmed on 9:03 camera-free (46.19, 45.74; 19 October).
- **The side photograph alone:** the pillar screws' heads r 2.77 × 2.0 (110-20a; and so the heads of 42055.lb, 42055.ring, 42055.pil/pilb, where the dial-side ones are cut to 1.6 by estimate); the mounting ring's band and flange (110-40a: 95.9, 6.0, 10.4; its ratios to the bore checked on video, `ledger\ring_dial`); the train bridge's 3.1 and the heights 16.8-19.9 (110-19a; 42:56 and the other side views use the 16.8 as their scale, so they check proportions, not this); the escape wheel 0.95 under the bridge (110-18a: 42:56 agrees to 0.5); the balance lower bridge's slab 7.9-10.9 (110-27b: three side views agree within 1 mm; one photograph disagrees). The train's levels are no longer the side photograph's: the centre wheel's are measured on 36:46-36:49.5 (110-24), the third's inferred under it, the fourth's drawn above both its rough readings (110-23, -25).
- **Through PT, places only** (the layout ledger's): L.B (on which the balance lower setting, cap and screws sit; L.B stands, ±0.6, on 13:44's jewel, 19 October), L.Fu and L.Ba (110-39's two lower bushings; both hold on 34:30 and 14:45, 19 October).
- **Not photo-era, but a figure alone:** the detent's block front, screws and washer (110-7c's foot, 110-8, 110-13b to -17), the escape teeth's form (110-18a), the train-blocking screw's sizes (110-29a): Figs. 90, 14, 57-59, 110, checked by the videos only for arrangement. The detent's plan, spring, bracket, trip spring and block's top face (110-7a, -7b, -10, -12, -13a) are measured since 20 October 2026 (11:10-11:33, bundle adjustment).

The 18 October `photocompare.py` pilot (RR, "The Hamilton against photographs") could not fit either photograph under 1-2 mm, so neither photograph can now test any place in this figure.

### Disagreements

Where the manual, a figure, a catalogue and the photographs or videos disagree for this figure, and what the model follows now:

1. **The escapement's settings: the manual's adjustment figures against the running chronometers.** The settings are chosen to meet Ops. 84-88 and 97 as the manual defines them (since 20 October 2026 with the detent's measured lengths: lock 6.1° against "about 6°" (5.5° until then, which left no room for wear: `dL` 0.019, the owner's choice), let-off 7.6° ≥ 6°, overall 28.0° in 26-30°, drop 2.2°, roller shake 0.056 mm, horn clearance 0.25 mm, the jewels 88° apart) and to run at 255° (Sec. II's 1⅜-1½ turns) with `TF` 67 s (the owner's choice, memory). The videos: `acoustic.py` reads `s7VW3RiJ97E` at 205-212° (205° re-read on the 14 October model), `xDKFZaY88cM` 180-209° (rough), `We1dLNXiBj0` running about 295° after its start (unlocking to strike 6.2-6.4 ms, 2:11-2:53); the start fits `TF` 98-114 s (`swing.py`) or 104-160 s by the escapement's work (`startup.js --chase`), against 67; and the strike stops moving from about 217° where the model's tooth lands 6.2° later over the same swing (We1dLNXiBj0 1:23-1:59). **Follows the manual** (and the owner's `TF`); none of the videos measures the lock, let-off or drop themselves.
2. **The discharge jewel:** its reach 0.50 mm is the top of 6:58's 0.4-0.5, chosen with `rT` for Op. 87's middle; its length along the staff 0.7 against 6:58's 0.85-1.0; its back radius's size not given (BM 117A gives only that it is radiused). **Follows the manual's figures**; the length is an estimate against a reading.
3. **The trip spring's bracket and the spring's length:** *settled 20 October 2026, the length re-read 25 October 2026: 6.45 free, its tip hidden under the horn's tab, the bracket 0.65 nearer the foot (`E:\mc-video\oq\ledger\run2_detent`); the tip's 0.45 closed, the moving parts' 0.35 left open as the frames' focal length*: the bracket measured 1.05 tall on 11:08-11:26 (BM 117A's 2.4 is its leg across the detent), placed on the blade from the trip spring's free start; the spring 5.8 free (11:10-11:33), where 7.9 was drawn against 11:26's 8.6 ± 0.6 read whole. ## The train bridge and the pillar plate on the owner's traces (20 October 2026)

The owner's points on KLUwI2UUCMQ 23:30 (the train bridge face up), 9:03 (the plate's dial side) and 14:43.38 (its train side), `tools/traces/`, each named after the model's holes and mapped by `mfgfit.py match` (no rim held: a robust map on the holes alone).

- **23:30, the upper train bridge: 21 of 29 points on model holes, a homography on all 21, 0.27 mm rms (15 px/mm).** Every hole within 0.07-0.58 mm of the model: the centre and third bushings (h1 0.26, h17 0.28), the pillars' screw holes (h2 0.09, h21 0.22, h11 0.17), the far barrel-bridge screw's tapped hole (h19 0.23), the steady pins' holes (h3 0.09, h20 0.09), the worst h12 (1.59, 24.14) 0.58 and h14 (-1.47, 33.79) 0.40. The third's bushing 1.2-1.3 mm off `L.T` on the rim-held anchors (the handover's item 1) is that map's stretch, not the bridge: 0.28 here. The fusee notch along its own arc (30-180 deg about `L.Fu`, refined on the image): r 16.65-17.0, median 16.68 against `TB_NOTCH` 16.72; free, r 16.40 about a centre 0.44 from `L.Fu`. The cut round the barrel: r 19.40 about a centre 0.32 from `L.Ba` (`TB_CR` 20.0; 23:30 earlier 20.26, 13:49.5 19.69). The eight points unmatched lie on the middle opening's edge (the balance and fourth lobes), not on holes.
- **9:03, the dial side: 19 of 24 on holes, 0.6 mm rms.** Many of the points are screw heads and the lower train bridge's settings standing 2-6 mm above the face, which the map gives no weight; at this frame's accuracy the plate holds. The opening under the lower train bridge, from 4 unrefined points: r 11.6 about (-8.19, 14.55) against r 11.3 about `L.T` (34:30 and 40:08 read 11.2-11.4): holds.
- **14:43.38, the train side: 23 of 27 identified, 1.0 mm rms.** Strongly oblique, the pillar tops 16.8 mm up among the points: good for naming the holes, not for sub-millimetre readings.
- **The dial feet's radius (45.9) stays open:** found automatically on 9:03, only 4 of the flange's 8 holes match among 39 round features seen through the bore. The owner's task 06 (`E:\mc-video\oq\measure\06_mounting_ring`) traces them.
